# ======== the sprite lists, the boot of the video, unpacking, fades, coins, bookkeeping and sound (naming batch I) ========
00F04E  func  draw_object_one_piece
00F104  func  draw_object_entry
00F126  func  draw_object_command
00F15C  func  draw_object_smoothed
00F396  func  finish_sprite_list
010243  func  note_road_crest
010634  func  init_screen
010688  func  clear_screen_ram
0106AF  func  init_video
01070C  func  reset_tile_layers
010741  func  clear_video_ram
01076A  func  clear_text_layer
0107BC  func  init_sprite_chip
0107F0  func  init_sprite_lists
010889  func  clear_sprite_group_count
010890  func  init_mixer
0108EA  func  init_io_chips
010936  func  load_sprite_ram_tables
010DC4  func  unpack
010E42  func  unpack_queued_step
010FA1  func  unpack_tile_pages
011018  func  init_coins
01106C  func  update_coins
0111CB  func  pulse_coin_meter
011213  func  write_bookkeeping_credits
01122C  func  add_service_credit
011252  func  insert_coin
0112D9  func  show_title_on_credit
0112F0  func  bookkeeping_increment16
011324  func  bookkeeping_add16
011341  func  bookkeeping_add32
01137D  func  bookkeeping_set16
01139E  func  bookkeeping_clear_credits
0113C0  func  bookkeeping_check
0113E5  func  bookkeeping_reset
01140E  func  bookkeeping_copy
011418  func  bookkeeping_update_checksum
01143E  func  bookkeeping_checksum
011594  func  upload_boot_palettes
01167E  func  step_screen_fade
0117E4  func  load_text_font
011868  func  print_text_layer_list
011874  func  print_text_layer
01190C  func  print_hex_byte
01192B  func  print_hex_digits
011A72  func  draw_text_sprites
011C60  func  clear_objects
011E88  func  start_sound_program
011FE6  func  fade_out_music
011FEA  func  fade_out_music_at
012004  func  reset_screen_sound
comment draw_object_one_piece Draw mode 8: the first piece of the animation's frame at its own size (no zoom, no flip), at x, y on the screen (less sprite_origin_x, sprite_origin_y), with the first word of its palette (rom_032BA4), into the sprite list and its halfway copy alike.
comment draw_object_entry Draw mode 1: copies a ready 16-byte sprite entry, from the address in fields 38-3B (animation and palette read as one 32-bit address), into the sprite list and its halfway copy, with the screen's bit in its fourth word (sprite_screen_bit). Not run in the scenarios.
comment draw_object_command Draw mode 6: copies a ready 16-byte entry from the address in fields 38-3B into the sprite list and its halfway copy as it is, without the screen's bit: for list commands such as crest_clip_update's clip.
comment draw_object_smoothed Draw mode A: every piece of the animation's frame at x, y on the screen (less sprite_origin_x, sprite_origin_y), zoomed by width_scale and height_scale, flipped by flags bits 6-7 (with bit 7 the pieces' offsets are negated, rom_00F4EC), each with its palette word; the halfway copy (+3000) is drawn from the averages of this frame's x, y, width_scale and height_scale and the last frame's, kept in the ring of sprite places (next_sprite_place, 16 bytes a record; the object's field 50 points at its record) when flags bit 5 says it was drawn the frame before. Keeps this frame's values in a new record and sets flags bit 5.
comment finish_sprite_list Finishes the screen's sprite list in the vertical blank (int_vector_40): picks a record of rom_00F508 by the screen (index) and sprite_list_buffers; when groups were drawn (sprite_groups), chains the depth lists from layer 0 to 415 (depth_lists: the list's start entry jumps to the first layer's newest group, each layer's tail to the next layer's head; the heads cleared), in the list and its halfway copy, and ends the chain with the record's word (a jump on to sprite entry 3, screen 2's list, after screen 1's; entry 4, the end, after screen 2's); points sprite RAM entries 2 and 3 (the jumps to each screen's list) at the halfway copy of the list just finished and at the other screen's finished list; then the next buffer (sprite_list_buffers, sprite_list_last, sprite_list_limit) and sprite_groups 0.
comment init_sprite_chip Sets up the sprite chip at boot: its control registers 500000-50000C from 010B0A-010B0F (rendering every frame), the buffer's flip bits inverted when the screen is flipped (flip_screen), then init_sprite_lists.
comment init_sprite_lists Sets up the sprite lists: sprite RAM entries 0-4 (0 clips to the whole screen, 1 sets the offset sprites can add, x A0 y DE (sprite_origin_x, sprite_origin_y), and jumps to 2; 2 and 3, the jumps to screen 1's and screen 2's lists, and 4 an end), an end at the start of the eight list buffers (404000, 40A000, 414000, 41A000 and their halfway copies 3000 on), each screen's list at its first buffer (sprite_list_last 404000 and 414000, sprite_list_buffers 0), its ring of sprite places at 20C000 and 21C000 (next_sprite_place), sprite_groups 0, and sprite_lists_set_up = 1 (nothing reads it).
comment clear_sprite_group_count Sets the count of sprite groups drawn this frame (sprite_groups) to 0: unless something is drawn after it, the vertical blank (finish_sprite_list) ends the screen's list at its start, without sprites. Used as the game modes start.
comment draw_text_sprites Writes a sprite per character of the string at R18 (closed by 0) into the sprite list and its halfway copy alike: words 0-3 from R1 and R2 (an 8x8 piece, with the screen's bit), the place R3 (y, x; x 8 more each character), the graphics R4 + 8 * the character, the attribute R5; then links them into depth layer 19E (screen 5678, drawn after nearly all the others) and counts them (sprite_groups). Returns the address after the string. The end of draw_text and draw_text_at.
comment load_sprite_ram_tables Copies data into sprite RAM outside the lists at boot: 1E0 bytes from 12297C to 410000, 2400 bytes from 107020 to 401000, and twelve tables of sixteen halfwords from 010C44 to 403400 (laid out as the indirect colour tables sprites can take from sprite RAM).
comment note_road_crest Notes a crest of the road while player_car_project_road projects it, for crest_clip_update: when it is the first, or higher on the screen (R27) than the last one noted, crest_count goes up, its depth layer R28 goes into crest_layers (at the old count, with 3 in the next place) and its height into crest_heights (at the new count; entry 0 is the bottom of the screen).
comment init_screen Sets up the constants of the screen's block at boot: index (0 for the block at 208000, 1 at 218000) and by it (rom_010BA0) sprite_screen_bit, palette_ram, mixer and io_chip; eye_distance, draw_distance (the second of the draw distances at 010BE2), sprite_origin_x, sprite_origin_y and view_pitch_base from 010BCC (010BC8 holds the draw distances' address); and stage_lengths: for each of the 31 roads of rom_03CC00 (the courses player_car_start_course starts by screen first_stage) the sum of its sections' lengths (the first halfword of each 6-byte record; the negative ones are commands: -1 goes on at the address it holds, -4 is 4 bytes long, -2 and -6 end it), and branch_road_length (14A) after them.
comment clear_screen_ram Clears the screen's work RAM at boot: E000 bytes from 8000 below its block (the objects and their lists) to 6000 into it.
comment init_mixer Sets the screen's mixer (registers 00-4E at mixer) and its two copies in the screen's block (mixer_values, mixer_defaults) to the defaults at 010AB6, with the other screen's two tile layers turned off (010B06: NBG1 and NBG3 on screen 1, NBG0 and NBG2 on screen 2; the two screens share video RAM, each showing two of the four layers).
comment init_io_chips Sets up the two I/O chips at boot: ports D, G and H outputs and cleared (the coin meters, lamps, LCD, the tile bank and the EEPROM's lines), each chip's screen on and the sound CPU out of reset (CNT 6 and 86), and the first bank of the wheels' and pedals' A/D inputs (C00060).
comment upload_boot_palettes Uploads three blocks of colours straight into the screen's palette RAM at boot (upload_palette): 256 colours at 9E00 (03153C), 16 at 9C00 (031740) and 2560 at 8000 (153904).
comment init_video Sets up video RAM: cleared (clear_video_ram), the text font (load_text_font), the video control register 31FF00 (0800; 0A00 flipped, flip_screen) and the registers 31FF02-31FF8F from 010B12, with their copy at video_registers, the four tile layers' scroll targets (scroll_targets-20E2AF) cleared, then reset_tile_layers.
comment reset_tile_layers Puts the screen's two tile layers (NBG0 and NBG2 for screen 1, NBG1 and NBG3 for screen 2) back at page 0 and scroll 0: their page selects (video_registers[32], video_registers[36]) and scroll targets (scroll_targets, scroll_targets[4]) cleared, and bits 12-15 of the video control register (31FF00, video_registers) cleared. Used as the game modes start.
comment clear_video_ram Clears video RAM below its registers (300000-31FEFF): the tilemap pages, the text layer and its font, the bitmap and the line tables.
comment clear_text_layer Clears the text layer's first 30 rows (64 cells each, 31F000-31FEFF).
comment load_text_font Copies the text layer's characters (3000 bytes, 384 characters of 8x8 pixels) from 104020 into video RAM at 318000.
comment print_text_layer Writes a string on the text layer: from the record R19, the cell's address (310000 + its first halfword), an attribute byte (the cells' high byte: palette) and the characters, closed by 0; returns the address after it.
comment print_hex_byte Never runs in the scenarios (ISDN only): writes a number's low byte as two hex digits on the text layer, ending before the cell it is given, with the attribute byte it is given (print_hex_digits).
comment print_hex_digits Writes a number in hex on the text layer, in as many cells as it is told, ending before the cell it is given, the lowest digit last: each digit a character ('0'-'9', 'A'-'F') with the attribute byte it is given; when the number runs out, the cells left before it are cleared (0). Only print_hex_byte runs it; the entries for four, six and eight digits before it (print_hex_halfword, print_hex_24_bits, print_hex_word) are unused.
comment print_text_layer_list Writes the strings of the list R19 on the text layer (print_text_layer each) until an FFFF halfword. Not run in the scenarios.
comment unpack Unpacks the packed data at R10 into R11: flag bits read the highest first, 8 to a byte as needed; 1 copies a byte; 01 takes a halfword (an offset back of its value >> 3, -1 to -8192, and a length of its low three bits + 2, or when they are 0 the next byte + 1; a 0 byte ends the data); 00 takes two bits more (a length of 2-5) and a byte (an offset back of -1 to -256); a length copies that many bytes from that far back in what has been unpacked.
comment unpack_queued_step Unpacks a little of the screen's queued tile pages (queue_tile_pages), from the frame loop's wait while unpack_state says there is work: with none under way, takes the next request of the ring unpack_queue (unpack_read_place; unpack_state 0 when it is empty); else goes on where it stopped, by unpack_state's second byte: FF one byte of a match (unpack_copy_source, unpack_copy_count), 1 one step of unpack's format (unpack_flags, unpack_flags_left, unpack_source, unpack_destination): a byte, or a match's first byte.
comment unpack_tile_pages Unpacks a list of tilemap pages into video RAM at once (unpack): each 32-bit entry a page number in its top byte (the page at 300000 + 400 * page) and the address of its packed data below, closed by a negative entry; the list format of queue_tile_pages.
comment step_screen_fade Steps the screen's fade while unk_94 bit 0 is set (vblank_update_mixer, every other vertical blank): a fade asked for by fade_kind (0 to black, 1 from black, 2 to white, 3 from white, 4 to a red tint, 5 back from it; records at 01173A) sets fade_level, fade_step, fade_end and fade_channels and fade_kind 6 (under way); then each time the level moves a step (6 bits: 20 is -32, black; 1F is +31, white) and goes into the mixer's colour offset 0 in mixer_values (registers 40-44: red, green, blue, those fade_channels has; register 3E's high byte C0), which vblank_update_mixer copies to the mixer; unk_94 bit 0 is cleared when the level reaches fade_end.
comment clear_objects Ends all of the screen's objects at once: every slot free (screen - 7FF0), both object lists empty (this frame's at - 7E00, the next one's at - 7D00, with run_objects' pointers to them at - 8000, - 7FFC, - 7FF4 and - 7FF8), and the queue of palette uploads (unk_900) emptied. At boot and as each game mode starts.
comment start_sound_program Starts the sound at boot: the screen's ring of sound requests with one request of sound 0 waiting (800 the place read, 801 the place written, the requests from 802), I/O chip 0's CNT 6 (its screen on, the sound CPU out of reset), screen byte 822 cleared, and 701FFF cleared, which lets the sound program's main loop run.
comment fade_out_music Fades out the screen's music with 20 (fade_out_music_at): a step every 4 x (20 + 1) of the sound program's ticks.
comment fade_out_music_at Fades out the screen's music: R0 into byte 18 of the screen's first track in the shared sound RAM (700918 for screen 1, 701118 for screen 2; rom_011FFC), which has the sound program lower the music's volume, from its last CC 10, by one every 4 x (R0 + 1) of its ticks; at 0 it stops the music's player and its MultiPCM voices (CC 50 7E). 20 from fade_out_music, 3F and 7F from car_radio_update, 7F from goal_update.
comment reset_screen_sound Sends sound command 1F straight to the screen's request byte in the shared sound RAM (700700 + 100 * screen, without going through the ring of requests) and mutes the car's engine (710 = 0, 712 bit 5, as set_tyre_noise does in the silent demo). As the game modes start. Command 1F plays song 03, then FF: song 03 sends CC 50 7F on channel E, which stops every MultiPCM voice of the screen, and FF clears the screen's eight sequence players (music and effects); FM notes go on.
comment update_coins The coins and service credits, in the vertical blank unless on free play (free_play): queues each seat's pending coin sound (4E, seat1_coin_sounds, seat2_coin_sounds) and service sound (50, seat1_service_sounds, seat2_service_sounds), one each vertical blank; SERVICE newly pressed (switches_pressed bits 0-1) gives that seat a credit (add_service_credit), counted in the bookkeeping's service credits (701F04); a coin chute let go (switches_released bits 2-3 seat 1's two chutes, 4-5 seat 2's) counts its coin (insert_coin, entries at 0114D4) and the credits it gives in the bookkeeping (701F02); then drives the coin meters (pulse_coin_meter: each seat's first, and its second unless the chutes are shared, chute_entry_offset).
comment pulse_coin_meter Drives coin meter R1 (its bit in the port D outputs at R12, port_d_outputs: bit 0 a seat's first chute's meter, bit 1 its second's) from its record R7 in coin_meters (frames on, frames off, pulses owed): when idle with a pulse owed, the bit goes on for 6 vertical blanks and off for 6. Then writes the credits into the bookkeeping and its backup (write_bookkeeping_credits) with the checksum.
comment write_bookkeeping_credits Writes the seats' credits (the halfwords at seat1_credits, seat1_bookkeeping_byte, seat2_credits, seat2_bookkeeping_byte) at R12: 701F74 in the bookkeeping or 701FF0 in its backup.
comment add_service_credit A service credit for the seat whose credits are at R10 (seat1_credits or seat2_credits): one credit more, up to 9 (at 9 its coin units are cleared; beyond, nothing); a credit given brings the screen R12 to the title from the attract mode (show_title_on_credit), asks for the seat's service sound (R11) and counts one in R9, which it returns. Not run in the scenarios.
comment insert_coin A coin from the chute of entry R19 (when its bit is in R6): takes the entry's variant (chute_entry_offset: with the chutes shared, the second counts as the first), writes its byte to I/O C00074, owes the chute's coin meter a pulse (coin_meters) and counts the coin in the bookkeeping (701F06-701F0C, bookkeeping_increment16); with fewer than 9 credits: the screen's coin sound, and the chute's coin units (first_chute_multiplier or second_chute_multiplier) go toward a bonus unit (the credit record's third byte; one more unit each bonus_adder, when set) and toward a credit (its second byte, seat1_coin_units: a credit each coins_per_credit, up to 9, show_title_on_credit). Returns R9 plus the credits given.
comment show_title_on_credit A credit on the screen R12 during the attract mode (game modes 1-5, the low byte of its unk_80) brings it to the title: unk_80 = 2 (game mode 2, game_mode_title, from its first step).
comment init_coins Sets up the coins: chute_entry_offset 14 (the two chutes shared: the second's coins counted as the first's, on its meter) when not on free play and both chutes give the same units (first_chute_multiplier = second_chute_multiplier), else 0; the seats' credits back from the bookkeeping (701F74-701F7A, bytes); and the low four bits of I/O chip 1's port F, inverted, into second_io_chip_port_f (nothing reads it).
comment bookkeeping_checksum Returns the checksum of the 3E halfwords at R2 (the bookkeeping or its backup): from 1, each halfword XORed in, then a shift right by one, with 8810 XORed in when the bit shifted out was 1.
comment bookkeeping_update_checksum Writes the checksum of the bookkeeping (701F02-701F7D in the shared sound RAM) into 701F00, after a change to it; returns it.
comment bookkeeping_copy Copies 3E halfwords from R2 over R3 (the bookkeeping and its backup at 701F7E, either way, or the defaults), then bookkeeping_update_checksum.
comment bookkeeping_check Checks the bookkeeping at boot: when the block (701F02) matches the checksum (701F00), copies it over the backup (701F7E); else when the backup matches, the backup over the block; else resets both (bookkeeping_reset).
comment bookkeeping_reset Resets the bookkeeping: the defaults at 011458 into the block (701F02), then the block into its backup (701F7E), with the checksum.
comment bookkeeping_increment16 Adds one to the 16-bit bookkeeping counter at R7 (and its backup's, 7C on) unless it is at FFFF, with the checksum: the coins by chute (insert_coin), the games by seat and the races' results (game_mode_play).
comment bookkeeping_add16 Adds R9 to the 16-bit bookkeeping counter at R7 (the credits from coins, 701F02, and the service credits, 701F04: update_coins) and to its backup's (7C on), with the checksum; past FFFF it stays at FFFF (and the backup's is left as it was: the code writes R7 twice).
comment bookkeeping_add32 Adds R9 to the 32-bit bookkeeping counter at R7 and to its backup's (7C on), with the checksum; past FFFFFFFF it stays there (the backup's left as it was): the play time by seat (game_mode_play).
comment bookkeeping_set16 Sets the 16-bit bookkeeping value at R7 and its backup's (7C on) to R9, with the checksum: the longest and shortest play (701F20, 701F22; game_mode_play).
comment bookkeeping_clear_credits Clears the credits kept in the bookkeeping (701F74-701F7B) and in its backup (701FF0-701FF7), with the checksum. Not run in the scenarios.

01DDAC  func  test_mode_coin_assignments_draw
01DE56  func  test_mode_coin_assignments_next_setting
comment test_mode_start Enters the test mode (reset calls it once when test_mode_on says the test mode is on): turns the link board off, clears the tilemaps and the sprite lists (init_video, init_sprite_lists), clears both screens' pending tile unpacking (screen unpack_state), demo flag (unk_90 bit 8) and unk_94 bit 0, the mixers' registers 40-4A, turns both screens on (the I/O chips' CNT), sets up the test mode's palettes and font (test_mode_set_up_colours), test_mode_flags 0 (INDIVIDUAL) and the cursor on EXIT, stops both monitors' sounds (command 1F through the sound command queue, test_mode_queue_sound and test_mode_queue_sound_screen_2), turns the lamps and the wheel motor off (checkpoint_lamp-wheel_motor[1], screen button_lamps, port_d_outputs, port_g_outputs), writes " LCD status OK! " on both cabinets' LCDs (the 16 bytes at F10 of each screen's block), sets test_mode_routine to test_mode_menu and reads the controls (test_mode_read_controls).
comment test_mode_frame The test mode's frame, which reset runs every video frame while the test mode is on: reads the buttons and the analog inputs (test_mode_read_controls), sends the sound board the next queued sound command (test_mode_send_sound) and runs test_mode_routine, the step of the menu or page being shown.
comment test_mode_menu Shows the test mode's menu, where every page returns: in CONTINUE mode (test_mode_flags bit 0) the cursor first moves on to the next item; clears the text layer, shows the E2PROM write count while SERVICE is held (test_mode_menu_show_write_count), draws TEST MODE, the heading (INDIVIDUAL or CONTINUE), the twelve items and SELECT BY SERVICE BUTTON AND PUSH TEST BUTTON, puts the cursor on test_mode_cursor (test_mode_set_cursor) and goes on to test_mode_menu_run. With the cursor on the heading (after test_mode_menu_toggle_continue has redrawn it) it goes straight on.
comment test_mode_menu_run The menu, each frame: SERVICE moves the cursor (test_mode_cursor) down, from EXIT back to the heading; TEST starts the item's page (rom_01C45C: test_mode_menu_toggle_continue, test_mode_memory_test, test_mode_volume_adjustment, test_mode_input_test, test_mode_output_test, test_mode_sound_test, test_mode_crt_test, test_mode_game_assignments, test_mode_coin_assignments, then the network assignments, bookkeeping, backup data clear and exit pages, test_mode_network_assignments_start, test_mode_bookkeeping_start, test_mode_backup_data_clear_start, test_mode_exit); the cursor's arrow blinks (test_mode_blink_address, test_mode_blink_timer: 11 frames shown, 6 hidden).
comment test_mode_menu_show_write_count While SERVICE is held as the menu is drawn: E2PROM WRITE COUNT and the count (eeprom_write_count, 5 digits) at the bottom of the menu.
comment test_mode_menu_toggle_continue The menu's heading: TEST switches between INDIVIDUAL and CONTINUE (test_mode_flags bit 0) and redraws it, then back to test_mode_menu. In CONTINUE mode each return to the menu moves the cursor to the next item, so the pages run one after another with TEST alone.
comment test_mode_memory_test The MEMORY TEST, all at once with the interrupts off and the sound program held in its wait loop (sound RAM 1FFF = 1): each chip's result shows TEST while it is tested, then GOOD or BAD (memory_test_results, test_mode_memory_test_draw). The program ROM's two halves by test_mode_memory_test_checksum against the sums at 03FFF8-03FFFF (IC37, IC40: 000000-03FFF7; IC36, IC39: 100000-1FEFFF); the video RAM (IC60, IC58, IC57, IC54) and the sprite RAM (IC61, IC67, IC68, IC70) by 4-bit lanes (test_mode_memory_test_nibbles); the palette RAMs' four 16 KB blocks by byte (IC1, IC3, IC2, IC4, IC120, IC121, IC122, IC123; test_mode_memory_test_halfwords); the work RAM (IC115-IC118, test_mode_memory_test_words) and the sound RAM (IC21, test_mode_memory_test_bytes) each saved in the sprite RAM and put back. The tilemaps, sprite lists and palettes it overwrites are set up again (init_video, init_sprite_lists, test_mode_set_up_colours). IC70 always shows GOOD: the test of its lane has no branch to BAD. Ends with the blinking PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT (test_mode_show_exit_prompt) and test_mode_memory_test_run.
comment test_mode_memory_test_run The memory test's results, each frame: TEST goes back to the menu; PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT blinks (test_mode_blink_prompt).
comment test_mode_memory_test_draw Draws the memory test's page (MEMORY TEST, 837-8890 <ROM> and its four chips, 837-8676 <RAM> and its 21, NOW TESTING) and each chip's result from memory_test_results beside its name: ---- (not yet), TEST, GOOD or BAD.
comment test_mode_memory_test_checksum Sums a stretch of the program ROM as two 16-bit checks, one of the even halfwords (the chip of the low half of each 32-bit word: IC37 or IC36) and one of the odd (IC40 or IC39): each halfword is xored in, the sum shifted right by one and xored with 8810 when a 1 falls out (a CRC); both start at 1.
comment test_mode_memory_test_words Tests RAM 32 bits at a time (the work RAM, in place: it takes its return address off the stack into a register and jumps back through it, so that it needs no stack in the RAM it overwrites): 8 passes, each writing a walking pattern (01010101 the first pass, one bit further each pass, turned by a few bits from word to word by the word's address) and reading it back; returns the bits found wrong (each mismatch's expected ^ read, xored together), a byte per chip; NOW TESTING blinks once a pass (test_mode_memory_test_blink).
comment test_mode_memory_test_halfwords test_mode_memory_test_words 16 bits at a time (the palette RAMs: a chip for each byte), with the pattern 0101; returns the bits found wrong.
comment test_mode_memory_test_bytes test_mode_memory_test_words a byte at a time (the sound RAM, all but its last byte, which holds the sound program), with the pattern 01; returns the bits found wrong.
comment test_mode_memory_test_nibbles test_mode_memory_test_words for RAM of 4-bit chips, 16 bits at a time (the video RAM and the sprite RAM): 4 passes with the pattern 1111 (a bit per chip); returns the bits found wrong, a nibble per chip.
comment test_mode_memory_test_blink Shows or hides NOW TESTING (each of its cells xored with its character in palette 66): the RAM tests call it after each pass.
comment test_mode_volume_adjustment Starts the VOLUME ADJUSTMENT, the calibration of the wheels' and pedals' potentiometers: draws ADJUST and CANCEL with the cursor on CANCEL (test_mode_page_cursor 1), the six controls' names (MONITOR A and B: STEERING WHEEL, ACCELERATOR, BRAKE) and the prompt; then test_mode_volume_adjustment_select.
comment test_mode_volume_adjustment_select Each frame: the bars (test_mode_volume_adjustment_draw); SERVICE moves the cursor between ADJUST and CANCEL; TEST on CANCEL goes back to the menu; TEST on ADJUST starts the adjustment: ADJUSTING, PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT, each control's range (volume_ranges) narrowed to where it is now, then test_mode_volume_adjustment_adjust.
comment test_mode_volume_adjustment_adjust The adjustment, each frame: each control's range (volume_ranges) widens to take its position (test_mode_analog_inputs), as the operator turns the wheels and presses the pedals all the way; the bars; TEST marks the settings changed (test_mode_flags bit 1) and goes back to the menu; PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT blinks.
comment test_mode_volume_adjustment_draw Draws the six controls' bars (32 cells, a cell for 8 steps): the range of volume_ranges in green and the control's position (test_mode_analog_inputs) in red.
comment test_mode_input_test Starts the INPUT TEST: clears the text layer, draws the inputs' names and the blinking PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT, then test_mode_input_test_run.
comment test_mode_input_test_run The input test, each frame: the inputs (test_mode_input_test_draw); TEST goes on to test_mode_input_test_release, so that the press shows as TEST On before the page ends; PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT blinks (test_mode_blink_prompt).
comment test_mode_input_test_release The input test once TEST is pressed: the inputs each frame until TEST is let go, then back to the menu.
comment test_mode_input_test_draw Draws the inputs of both monitors (cabinets): the wheel, accelerator and brake as the game sees them through the calibration (test_mode_analog_inputs clamped to volume_ranges and scaled to 0-FF), as a mark on a bar of 16 cells and a hexadecimal number; SHIFT UP, SHIFT DOWN, START, DJ/MUSIC, <<, >>, COIN and SERVICE On or Off, read from each I/O chip's ports (active low); TEST On when either monitor's is pressed.
comment test_mode_output_test Starts the OUTPUT TEST: the ten outputs of the two monitors (cabinets) with their state (test_mode_output_test_draw), EXIT and the prompt, the cursor on EXIT (test_mode_page_cursor 10); then test_mode_output_test_run.
comment test_mode_output_test_run The output test, each frame: TEST turns the output under the cursor on and letting go of it turns it off again (test_mode_output_test_toggle both times), so it is on while TEST is held; TEST on EXIT runs test_mode_output_test_exit; SERVICE, while TEST is not held, moves the cursor through the eleven items; the arrow blinks.
comment test_mode_output_test_draw Draws On or Off for the ten outputs, from their bits in the game's output bytes: monitor A's CHECK POINT and RACE LEADER lamps (port_d_outputs bits 1 and 3), the steering wheel's motor (port_d_outputs bit 4), the DJ/MUSIC and << >> lamps (port_g_outputs bits 0 and 1); monitor B's the same bits of 20E093 and 20E095.
comment test_mode_output_test_toggle Turns the output under the cursor (test_mode_page_cursor) on or off: flips its bit in the game's output bytes (the list at 01D5C0).
comment test_mode_output_test_exit The output test's EXIT: turns the lamps and the wheel motor off (port_d_outputs &= E0E1, port_g_outputs &= FBFC: monitor B's DJ/MUSIC and << >> bits, 20E095 bits 0-1, are not among them) and goes back to the menu.
comment test_mode_sound_test Starts the SOUND TEST: MONITOR A No., MONITOR B No. (the numbers of the next sounds, both 0: sound_test_number_a, sound_test_number_b), EXIT with the cursor on it (test_mode_page_cursor 2) and the prompt; then test_mode_sound_test_run.
comment test_mode_sound_test_run The sound test, each frame: SERVICE moves the cursor through the three items; TEST stops both monitors' sounds (command 1F to each), then plays monitor A's next sound (test_mode_sound_test_play_a), monitor B's (test_mode_sound_test_play_b) or goes back to the menu (test_mode_sound_test_exit), and redraws the numbers; the arrow blinks.
comment test_mode_sound_test_draw Draws the two monitors' sound numbers (sound_test_number_a, sound_test_number_b), 3 digits each.
comment test_mode_sound_test_play_a Plays monitor A's sound number sound_test_number_a from the list of 121 sound codes at 01D7C6 (test_mode_queue_sound) and steps to the next; from number 40 on it first sends command D6 (DA from 66, D8 from 6B), which selects sound set 6 (A, 8): the music parts' templates and the screen's MultiPCM sample bank; past the list's end (its closing 0) the number goes back to 0 without a sound.
comment test_mode_sound_test_play_b test_mode_sound_test_play_a for monitor B: sound_test_number_b, sent through test_mode_queue_sound_screen_2.
comment test_mode_sound_test_exit The sound test's EXIT: back to the menu.
comment test_mode_crt_test Starts the C.R.T. TEST's first page: a black background (the background colour of both palettes, 609C00 and 689C00), C.R.T. TEST 1/2, the colour bars (test_mode_crt_test_draw_bars) and the blinking PUSH TEST BUTTON TO CONTINUE; then test_mode_crt_test_bars_run.
comment test_mode_crt_test_bars_run The colour bars, each frame: TEST goes on to the second page (test_mode_crt_test_grid); the message blinks.
comment test_mode_crt_test_grid The C.R.T. test's second page: the crosshatch (test_mode_crt_test_draw_grid), C.R.T. TEST 2/2 and the blinking PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT; then test_mode_crt_test_grid_run.
comment test_mode_crt_test_grid_run The crosshatch, each frame: TEST clears the text layer, gives the background back its blue (733) and goes back to the menu; the message blinks.
comment test_mode_crt_test_draw_bars Draws the first page on the text layer: a frame around the screen and four bars of 32 steps (test_mode_crt_test_draw_bar, characters 8001-8004: red, green, blue and white).
comment test_mode_crt_test_draw_bar Draws one colour bar: 5 rows of 32 cells of character R0, each column with the next palette (the cell + 200), so that the colour steps up across it; returns the place 5 rows down for the next bar.
comment test_mode_crt_test_draw_grid Draws the crosshatch of the second page over the whole text layer (characters 6C1B-6C1F: the edges and the inside), and blanks the cell after the exit message.
comment test_mode_game_assignments Starts the GAME ASSIGNMENTS: the settings' names and values (test_mode_game_assignments_draw), EXIT, the prompt, the cursor on EXIT (test_mode_page_cursor 9); then test_mode_game_assignments_run.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_run The game assignments, each frame: SERVICE moves the cursor through the ten items; TEST changes the setting under it: the game difficulty (test_mode_game_assignments_difficulty), a stage's time (test_mode_game_assignments_time), the steering wheel reaction (test_mode_game_assignments_steering), the advertise sound (test_mode_game_assignments_advertise), or EXIT (test_mode_game_assignments_exit); the arrow blinks.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_draw Draws the game settings: game_difficulty + 1 (1-8) and its bar from EASY to HARD (two cells a level), the six stages' time_difficulty (NORMAL, or +n SEC / -n SEC), and steering_wheel_reaction and advertise_sound (bit 0) as On or Off.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_difficulty GAME DIFFICULTY: game_difficulty one level harder, after the eighth back to the first; redraws.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_time TIME DIFFICULTY: the time_difficulty of the stage under the cursor (items 1-6: PRE-STAGE, STAGE 1-4, FINAL STAGE) one second more, from +5 back to -5; redraws.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_steering STEERING WHEEL REACTION: steering_wheel_reaction on or off (1 or 0); redraws.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_advertise ADVERTISE SOUND: advertise_sound on or off (1 or 0); redraws.
comment test_mode_game_assignments_exit The game assignments' EXIT: compares game_difficulty, time_difficulty, steering_wheel_reaction and advertise_sound with their backup copy 3C bytes on (backup_settings_checksum on, the copy of the settings the boot code keeps); if any changed, marks the settings changed (test_mode_flags bit 1); back to the menu.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments Starts the COIN ASSIGNMENTS with the cursor on EXIT (test_mode_page_cursor 2): test_mode_coin_assignments_show.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_show Draws the coin assignments page (COIN/CREDIT SETTING with the setting, test_mode_coin_assignments_draw; MANUAL SETTING; EXIT; the prompt) with the cursor where test_mode_page_cursor says: on EXIT when the page starts, on MANUAL SETTING when that page (test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_start) returns here; then test_mode_coin_assignments_run.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_run The coin assignments, each frame: SERVICE moves the cursor through the three items; TEST on COIN/CREDIT SETTING takes the next setting (test_mode_coin_assignments_next_setting), on MANUAL SETTING opens that page (test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_start), on EXIT leaves (test_mode_coin_assignments_exit); the arrow blinks. Its table's last four entries belong to the manual setting page, whose table starts inside this one: they are never taken from here.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_draw Draws the coin/credit setting: its number (coin_setting + 1) and what it gives, from its description (rom_01E220): up to five lines of "n COIN(S) m CREDIT(S)", or a text (FREE PLAY, MANUAL SETTING).
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_next_setting COIN/CREDIT SETTING: the next of the 26 settings (coin_setting, after FREE PLAY back to #1): its record at 01E3E8 gives coins_per_credit, bonus_adder, first_chute_multiplier and second_chute_multiplier, and free_play 0; FREE PLAY (#26) sets coins_per_credit, first_chute_multiplier and second_chute_multiplier to 1 and bonus_adder and free_play to FF. Settings whose first three bytes are the ones in place are skipped (they differ only in second_chute_multiplier), so only 15 of them come up: #1, 6, 8-12, 15, 17-19, 21, 22, 24, 26. Redraws.

comment test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_start Opens the coin assignments' MANUAL SETTING page: clears the text, draws its texts (COIN TO CREDIT, BONUS ADDER, MULTIPLIER, the table of credits for 1 to 9 coins, EXIT) and the setting (test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_draw), the instructions, puts the cursor on EXIT (test_mode_page_cursor 3) and goes on in test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_run.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_run Each frame of the MANUAL SETTING page: SERVICE moves the cursor down its four lines (test_mode_page_cursor, wrapping), TEST changes the line's value: COIN TO CREDIT (test_mode_coin_assignments_step_coin_to_credit), BONUS ADDER (test_mode_coin_assignments_step_bonus_adder), MULTIPLIER (test_mode_coin_assignments_step_multiplier) or EXIT (test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_exit); then blinks the cursor.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_draw Draws the manual coin setting: coins_per_credit (N COIN(S) 1 CREDIT), the bonus_adder (N COINS GIVE 1 EXTRA COIN, NO BONUS ADDER, or FREE PLAY when negative), the first_chute_multiplier (1 COIN COUNTS AS N COIN(S)), and the table of what 1 to 9 coins give: each coin counts as the multiplier, the bonus adds a coin for every bonus_adder counted (the rest carried on), and the credits (at most 9) show as two digits (test_mode_draw_two_digits) with what is left over as a fraction of a credit in the font's fraction characters.
comment test_mode_draw_two_digits Draws R5 (below 100) at text cell R10 as two digits in white (6A), a blank for a 0 tens digit, and returns the cell after them. Used by test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_draw.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_step_coin_to_credit COIN TO CREDIT: test_mode_coin_assignments_step_value with coins_per_credit.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_step_value Steps the coin setting R0 bytes into the settings (coins_per_credit or first_chute_multiplier) from 1 to 9 and round; unless free play is on, the coin setting becomes the manual one (coin_setting 1A); redraws (test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_draw).
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_step_bonus_adder BONUS ADDER: steps bonus_adder through none (0), 2 to 9 and back to none; from free play (FF) it goes to none and turns free_play off; the coin setting becomes the manual one (coin_setting 1A); redraws.
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_step_multiplier MULTIPLIER: test_mode_coin_assignments_step_value with first_chute_multiplier (1 COIN COUNTS AS N COIN).
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_manual_exit EXIT of the MANUAL SETTING page: back to the coin assignments page, its cursor on MANUAL SETTING (test_mode_page_cursor 1).
comment test_mode_coin_assignments_exit EXIT of the coin assignments page: when coins_per_credit, first_chute_multiplier, bonus_adder, free_play or coin_setting differ from the copy last written to the EEPROM (3C bytes on), marks the settings changed (test_mode_flags bit 1, written at the test mode's EXIT) and clears the credits kept in the shared RAM (bookkeeping_clear_credits); back to the menu.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_start Opens the NETWORK ASSIGNMENTS page: its texts, with PRIVILEGE MODE and CABINET ID hidden (colour 6E) when the cabinet is stand_alone; test_mode_privilege_mode from link_master; the values (test_mode_network_assignments_draw), the instructions, the cursor on EXIT; goes on in test_mode_network_assignments_run.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_run Each frame of the NETWORK ASSIGNMENTS page: SERVICE moves the cursor (between COMMUNICATION and EXIT only when stand_alone, else down all four lines), TEST runs its line: COMMUNICATION (test_mode_network_assignments_toggle_communication), PRIVILEGE MODE (test_mode_network_assignments_toggle_privilege_mode), CABINET ID (test_mode_network_assignments_step_cabinet_id) or EXIT (test_mode_network_assignments_exit); then blinks the cursor.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_draw Draws the network settings: COMMUNICATION NETWORK or STAND-ALONE (stand_alone), PRIVILEGE MODE SLAVE or MASTER (test_mode_privilege_mode), CABINET ID 1-4 (cabinet_id + 1); the last two hidden (colour 6E) when stand-alone.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_toggle_communication COMMUNICATION: switches stand_alone (NETWORK, STAND-ALONE), puts the privilege mode back to MASTER (link_master 1, isdn_mode 0, test_mode_privilege_mode 1), shows or hides the PRIVILEGE MODE and CABINET ID labels and redraws the values.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_toggle_privilege_mode PRIVILEGE MODE: switches test_mode_privilege_mode between SLAVE and MASTER and sets link_master and isdn_mode from it (01E6E5: SLAVE 0 and 0, MASTER 1 and 0; the table's third pair, ISDN, 0 and 1, cannot be reached); redraws.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_step_cabinet_id CABINET ID: steps cabinet_id through 0-3 (shown 1-4); redraws.
comment test_mode_network_assignments_exit EXIT of the NETWORK ASSIGNMENTS page: when stand_alone, link_master, isdn_mode or cabinet_id differ from the copy last written to the EEPROM, marks the settings changed (test_mode_flags bit 1); back to the menu.
comment test_mode_bookkeeping_start Opens the first BOOKKEEPING page (1/2): its texts, its numbers (test_mode_bookkeeping_draw_totals) and the blinking PUSH TEST BUTTON TO CONTINUE (test_mode_show_continue_prompt); goes on in test_mode_bookkeeping_wait.
comment test_mode_bookkeeping_wait Blinks the prompt (test_mode_blink_prompt) until TEST, then the second page (test_mode_bookkeeping_second_page_start).
comment test_mode_bookkeeping_second_page_start Opens the second BOOKKEEPING page (2/2): its texts, the time histogram and the course records (test_mode_bookkeeping_draw_histogram) and the blinking PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT (test_mode_show_exit_prompt); goes on in test_mode_bookkeeping_second_page_wait.
comment test_mode_bookkeeping_second_page_wait Blinks the prompt until TEST, then back to the menu.
comment test_mode_bookkeeping_draw_totals Draws the first bookkeeping page's numbers from the bookkeeping in the sound board's shared RAM (701F00 on): COIN CHUTE A and B (the coins of each seat's first chute, 701F06 and 701F0A) and TOTAL COIN(S); COIN CREDIT(S) (701F02) and SERVICE CREDIT(S) (701F04) and their TOTAL; NUMBER OF GAME(S) A and B (701F14, 701F16); TOTAL TIME (701F10, counted by count_total_time) and GAME PLAY TIME A and B (701F18, 701F1C) in days, hours, minutes and seconds (test_mode_draw_duration); once a game was played, AVERAGE GAME TIME (the play times over the games), LONGEST (701F20) and SHORTEST (701F22) in minutes and seconds (test_mode_draw_minutes_seconds).
comment test_mode_bookkeeping_draw_histogram Draws the second bookkeeping page's numbers: the TIME HISTOGRAM, how far the games got (seven counts from 701F24: PRE-STAGE, STAGE 1-5, COMPLETE), each with a bar of up to 20 blocks scaled to the largest; and OutRunner of OutRunners, the record of each of the ten goals (AUSTRALIA to KENYA; six bytes each from 701F32: the car, none when negative, shown by its colour, the three letters of the name and the time in hundredths, shown as minutes, seconds and hundredths).
comment test_mode_draw_duration Draws a time of R0 seconds at text cell R11 as days (5 digits), hours, minutes and seconds (2 digits each, a cell apart, for the D H M S of the text) and returns the cell after it.
comment test_mode_draw_minutes_seconds Draws a time of R0 seconds at text cell R11 as minutes and seconds, 2 digits each a cell apart; R11 moves to the seconds.
comment test_mode_backup_data_clear_start Opens the BACKUP DATA CLEAR page: YES (CLEAR) and NO (CANCEL), the instructions, the cursor on NO; goes on in test_mode_backup_data_clear_run.
comment test_mode_backup_data_clear_run Each frame of the BACKUP DATA CLEAR page: SERVICE moves the cursor between YES and NO; TEST on NO goes back to the menu; TEST on YES leaves the cursor on, clears the bookkeeping (bookkeeping_reset: all of it back to its defaults at 011458, zeros but SHORTEST GAME TIME FFFF and no course records, the credits too), shows COMPLETED, hides SELECT BY SERVICE BUTTON and blinks PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT (test_mode_show_exit_prompt) until TEST (test_mode_backup_data_clear_wait).
comment test_mode_backup_data_clear_wait After the clear: blinks the prompt until TEST, then back to the menu.
comment test_mode_exit EXIT: when a page changed a setting (test_mode_flags bit 1, cleared here) writes the settings to the EEPROM (save_settings, which counts eeprom_write_count up to 10000); while eeprom_write_count is below 10000, or with nothing to write, leaves the test mode: test_mode_on 0 and the I/O chips' CNT registers 4 and 80 (the test mode's start wrote 6 and 86: bit 1, cleared now, turns a chip's screen output on, runtime/src/io.c); at 10000 shows the CAUTION that the EEPROM has been written 10,000 times and waits for TEST first (test_mode_exit_caution_wait).
comment test_mode_exit_caution_wait Blinks PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT under the CAUTION until TEST, then leaves the test mode as test_mode_exit does.
comment test_mode_set_up_colours Sets up the test mode's colours and tiles on both screens: the whole palette in the background's colour (733), the text palettes (01F304 for screen 1, 01F506 for screen 2: the text colours 60 grey, 62 red, 64 green, 66 yellow, 68 cyan, 6A white, 6E the background's), 32 palettes after them whose colours 1-4 are red, green, blue and white at levels 0-31, and four solid tiles of colours 1-4 (318020): what the C.R.T. test's colour bars are drawn with.
comment test_mode_draw_instructions Draws SELECT BY SERVICE BUTTON AND PUSH TEST BUTTON at the bottom of the screen.
comment test_mode_show_exit_prompt Makes PUSH TEST BUTTON TO EXIT (the text record at 01F2BC, row 25) the prompt test_mode_blink_prompt blinks (test_mode_blink_address), from the next frame.
comment test_mode_show_continue_prompt Makes PUSH TEST BUTTON TO CONTINUE (the text record at 01F2DE) the prompt test_mode_blink_prompt blinks, from the next frame.
comment test_mode_blink_prompt Blinks the prompt whose text record test_mode_blink_address holds: when test_mode_blink_timer runs out, draws it (shown 21 frames) if its first cell is empty, else erases it (hidden 11 frames).
comment test_mode_set_cursor Puts the cursor at text cell R10, the cell two before a menu line: test_mode_blink_address = R10 (the cursor, character 7F, blinks there: the menu loops' tail at 01F0AD), test_mode_blink_timer 0, and the line in white (6A, test_mode_colour_line).
comment test_mode_clear_cursor Takes the cursor off its line: clears its cell (test_mode_blink_address) and puts the line back in grey (60).
comment test_mode_draw_texts Draws the text record at R10 on the text layer: lines of a cell (halfword), characters and colour changes (a negative byte: its complement is the colour, the cells' high byte), each closed by 0; a 0 cell ends it.
comment test_mode_draw_count Draws the number R0 as test_mode_draw_number does, and R3 cells from R10 an S (or a blank for 1 or less) for the plural of the word beside it (COIN(S), CREDIT(S), GAME(S)); R1 holds the S's colour (the word's) in its low byte and the digits' in its second.
comment test_mode_draw_number Draws the number R0 in decimal in R2 cells ending at text cell R10 + 2 * R2, right-aligned in the colour in R1's low byte (the cells' high byte), the unused cells before it cleared.
comment test_mode_draw_hex_number test_mode_draw_number in hexadecimal (digits 0-9, A-F).
comment test_mode_colour_line Gives the text from cell R10 up to the next empty cell the colour R0 (60 grey, 6A white, 6E hidden...).
comment test_mode_clear_text Clears the text layer (30 rows of 64 cells).
comment test_mode_read_controls The test mode's controls, each frame: test_mode_buttons (SERVICE bit 0, TEST bit 1, START bit 4 and bit 5 of input port E, of either seat; bits 4-5 of port F count as SERVICE and TEST too), test_mode_buttons_previous, test_mode_buttons_pressed and test_mode_buttons_released; and the analog inputs into test_mode_analog_inputs, alternately (test_mode_flags bit 2, which also sets the converter's input bank): the first four (seat A's wheel, gas and brake, seat B's wheel) one frame, seat B's gas and brake the next.
comment test_mode_send_sound Sends the test mode's next queued sound request (test_mode_sound_queue, test_mode_sound_queue_read) to the sound CPU, one a frame: to screen 2's command byte (SOUND_COMMAND_2) when the request has its high byte set, else to screen 1's (SOUND_COMMAND_1).
comment test_mode_queue_sound_screen_2 test_mode_queue_sound for screen 2's sound CPU commands (the high byte set).
comment test_mode_queue_sound Puts sound request R0 in the test mode's ring of 16 (test_mode_sound_queue at test_mode_sound_queue_write) for test_mode_send_sound: screen 1's, or screen 2's with the high byte set (test_mode_queue_sound_screen_2).
comment vblank_update_mixer In the vertical blank, a screen's mixer and colours: while the screen's unk_94 bit 0 is set, a step of its colour offsets (step_screen_fade) into the mixer's colour offset registers (3E-4B); unk_94 bit 2 (cleared) sends the mixer settings at 160 of the screen (registers 00-3D and 4C-4F); unk_94 bit 3 (cleared) makes the set at 1B0 the current one and sends it; then the queued palettes (upload_queued_palettes).
comment vblank_update_scrolls In the vertical blank, the tilemaps' scrolls (two per screen): those of the screen whose task was running from video_registers[8] and video_registers[16] as they are; those of the other screen half-way between the values they have (video_registers[8], video_registers[16] by screen) and the new ones (scroll_targets, scroll_targets[4]), which become their values: the in-between frame of the screen whose task did not run (each task's scrolls change every other frame); and the byte at 31FF05 from video_registers[2]'s second byte, which steps by 4 (bits 2-3).
comment check_test_button Turns the test mode on (test_mode_on FF) when TEST has just been pressed (bit 7 of switches_pressed, the inputs newly pressed); the boot task's loop then runs the test mode.
comment send_sound_requests In the vertical blank, sends the screen's next sound request to the sound CPU: one from its ring of 32 (queue_sound's: sound_queue_read, sound_queue_write, the requests after them) to its command byte (SOUND_COMMAND_1 for screen 1, SOUND_COMMAND_2 for screen 2), and one from its second ring at 840 (second_sound_queue_read, second_sound_queue_write; only the unused request_second_sound fills it) to the byte 8 after it, which the sound program never reads.
comment count_total_time Counts the bookkeeping's TOTAL TIME (701F10, seconds, kept with its copy and checksum), outside the test mode only: total_time_count gains 17 every vertical blank and a second passes at each 1000 (2000 for every 73rd second).
comment update_lamps In the vertical blank, the cabinet's lamps: blinks each seat's CHECK POINT lamp (output port D bit 1: checkpoint_lamp, checkpoint_lamp_2) and RACE LEADER lamp (bit 3: leader_lamp, leader_lamp_2), each with a blinks, a period and a timer byte after it: while blinks is not 0 the timer counts the vertical blanks and at 0 toggles the lamp, starts again from period and takes one off blinks; writes output port D of each I/O chip from port_d_outputs or port_d_outputs[1] with those lamps and, when the seat is not in the attract mode (screen demo_flags bit 8) and the STEERING WHEEL REACTION setting (steering_wheel_reaction) is on, the wheel motor's bits (wheel_motor by seat); then the music and arrows lamps (update_button_lamps).
comment update_button_lamps A screen's two button lamps, in its port G image (R11: port_g_outputs or port_g_outputs[1], which the LCD's output sends): the DJ/MUSIC lamp (bit 0) by the low byte of button_lamps and the << >> lamp (bit 1) by its high byte: 0 off, negative on, n blinking every n vertical blanks (music_lamp_timer, arrows_lamp_timer), each mode taken when it changes (music_lamp_mode, arrows_lamp_mode).
comment int_vector_40 The vertical blank interrupt (IRQ 0), every video frame: counts vblank_counter and vblanks_since_start, starts timer 0 for the LCD (int_vector_43, 34 times: timer_interrupts_left); when it interrupted screen 2's task: both screens' mixers and palettes (vblank_update_mixer), the video registers (all 72 halfwords from video_registers when send_video_registers asks for them, which it clears, else the 8 at video_registers[16] and the 8 at video_registers[32]) and the external tile banks of chip 0's port H (2 bits each of tile_banks-tile_banks[3]); then for the interrupted task's screen the sprite list (finish_sprite_list), send_sound_requests, and vblank_update_scrolls unless the screen's skip_scroll_update is set (cleared here); outside the test mode the inputs (read_switches_and_analog), pack_seat_inputs (screen 1) or queue_seat_inputs (screen 2), check_test_button and, without free play, the coins (update_coins); then update_lamps, the LCD (lcd_refresh), count_total_time; sets frame_ready; swaps the tasks (task_swap) unless single_task is set, when task_swapping is set or when the test mode is on and screen 1's task is running; counts the frame for the running task's screen (screen_1_vblanks, screen_2_vblanks).
comment int_vector_41 The end of the vertical blank (IRQ 1): only acknowledged.
comment int_vector_42 The sound CPU's interrupt (IRQ 2): only acknowledged. The sound program never raises it.
comment int_vector_43 Timer 0 (IRQ 3), which int_vector_40 starts each frame at its shortest period: sends the LCD its next piece (lcd_send_next_nibble) and starts the timer again until timer_interrupts_left runs out, 34 times a frame. When it runs out it tests the screen's byte 1 and test_mode_on but goes the same way; the code after them (unused_03F7D8: counts debug_timer_rounds and shows it on the text layer) is never reached.
comment int_vector_44 Timer 1 (IRQ 4), the link board's: outside the ISDN mode (isdn_mode) only acknowledged, which leaves the timer stopped; in it counts link_wait_count and takes the kind of the link board's message (its byte at 800010, cleared as it is read): 3, a message, is copied out of the board (link_isdn_receive_records: 800600 on to 21E000), link_wait_count cleared and the timer started at 168; any other kind polls again at the shortest period. link_unused_case is a fourth case (the controls, pack_seat_inputs, then 96) its table does not list.
comment int_default The vectors no interrupt uses (250 of the 256): halt instructions, on into int_vector_02's.
comment int_vector_02 The NMI: halt instructions, then on into check_test_button (whose return is not an interrupt's).

# ======== the code nothing that runs reaches (naming batch U) ========
0010EB  func  bar_picture_bar_start
001141  func  bar_picture_bar_fly
0011AB  func  bar_picture_bar_draw
0107A0  func  clear_tile_page
017DB0  func  backdrop_far_update
018050  func  backdrop_flashing_update
01B1B1  func  kanji_text_viewer_draw_line
01B1CD  func  kanji_text_viewer_draw_character
01B1E7  func  kanji_text_viewer_draw_quarter
01B200  func  kanji_text_viewer_character_address
01B20C  func  kanji_text_viewer_draw_number
comment bar_picture_start Unused: starts the 81 bars of a picture, an object each (start_object, handler bar_picture_bar_start) from the 8-byte records at 0011B1 (ended by a negative first word): its place on the screen into target_x, target_y and its two scales into scales. The places make two rows across the screen (y 19-107 and 139-195); what the bars show cannot be told without drawing them.
comment bar_picture_bar_start Only unused code reaches it (bar_picture_start): sets up a bar of the picture: drawn on the screen (draw_mode 0) with animation 0 and palette 25, its width_scale and height_scale from scales, depth layer 3, at the middle of the screen (x A0, y 70), with a random speed_x and speed_y (-8 to 7: a byte of RANDOM_D80000 shifted right 4); then bar_picture_bar_fly.
comment bar_picture_bar_fly Only unused code reaches it (bar_picture_start): each frame the bar's speed_x and speed_y grow by 1 toward its place (target_x, target_y) and it moves by them, swinging about it; an axis whose next step lands exactly on the place is put there for that frame. When both land on the same frame it shows frame D and stays (bar_picture_bar_draw). Drawn every frame (draw_object).
comment bar_picture_bar_draw Only unused code reaches it (bar_picture_start): the bar in its place: draws it (draw_object) each frame.
comment route_map_leg_car_end Unused: ends the object (flags 0). It follows the code of route_map_leg_car_show's last state (draw_object), whose state table lists only states 0 and 1.
comment lap_timer_spare_return Unused: does nothing (a lone return after lap_timer_update's last jump to draw_object).
comment stage_entry_spare_return Unused: does nothing (a second return after stage_entry_update's).
comment time_to_text_thousandths Unused: time_to_text for a time in thousandths of a second: R0 / 10 (into hundredths), then time_to_text at R10 (it runs on into it).
comment network_check_delay Unused: a busy wait: counts 3000 down to 0 and returns. It sits between network_check_wait_isdn and network_check_greeting.
comment clear_tile_pages Unused: clears the tilemap pages from the one at R9 (its offset in video RAM) down to the first, 400 bytes apart (clear_tile_page each).
comment clear_tile_page Only unused code reaches it (clear_tile_pages): clears the tilemap page at R9 (300000 + R9: 200 halfwords copied from the zeros at 100800).
comment unrolled_copy Unused: meant to copy R0 & 3F8 bytes from R10 to R11 by jumping into a run of 8-byte moves at 0109B4, but the jump (200 less half the count from the run's start) is worked out for 128 moves and the run has 64: it copies the count less 200 bytes, nothing for 200, and for less it jumps past the run into the data at 010AB5.
comment unpack_list Unused: unpacks a list of packed blocks at once (unpack): 8-byte entries, the packed data's address and where to unpack it, closed by a negative entry (the first is taken without the check). unpack_tile_pages does the same for a list of tile pages.
comment queue_unpack_list Unused: queues the entries of an unpack_list list (the packed data's address, where to unpack it; closed by a negative entry, the first taken unchecked) as they are in the screen's ring unpack_queue (unpack_write_place), with unpack_state set to say there is work, for unpack_queued_step. queue_tile_pages does the same for a list of tile pages.
comment bookkeeping_increment8 Unused: adds one to the 8-bit bookkeeping counter at R7 (and its backup's, 7C on) unless it is at FF, with the checksum (bookkeeping_update_checksum).
comment bookkeeping_add8 Unused: adds R9 to the 8-bit bookkeeping counter at R7 and to its backup's (7C on), with the checksum; past FF it stays at FF (and the backup's is left as it was: the code writes R7 twice), as bookkeeping_add16 does.
comment bookkeeping_set32 Unused: sets the 32-bit bookkeeping value at R7 and its backup's (7C on) to R9, with the checksum.
comment bookkeeping_set8 Unused: sets the 8-bit bookkeeping value at R7 and its backup's (7C on) to R9, with the checksum.
comment bookkeeping_check_block Unused: checks the bookkeeping block (701F02) against its checksum (701F00) and resets both it and its backup (bookkeeping_reset) when they differ; unlike bookkeeping_check it does not try the backup.
comment load_shadowed_text_font Unused: builds the text layer's first 128 characters (318000, where load_text_font puts its 384) from the one-bit font at 103C20 (8 bytes a character, a bit a pixel): the character's pixels in pen 1, and a shadow in pen 2 one pixel right and one row down under them.
comment erase_text_layer_to_blank_character Unused: erases a string from the text layer: from the cell of the print_text_layer record R19, writes 0 cells until the next cell's character is 0.
comment print_text_layer_halfwords Unused: print_text_layer with the characters as halfwords: from the record R19, the cell's address (310000 + its first halfword), the attribute byte, then the characters, 16 bits each (ORed with the attribute << 8, so they can reach past 255: the font has 384), closed by a 0 halfword.
comment erase_text_layer_to_blank_cell Unused: erases a string from the text layer as erase_text_layer_to_blank_character does, but until the next cell is all 0 (character and attribute).
comment print_hex_halfword Unused: writes a number's low halfword as four hex digits on the text layer, ending before the cell it is given, with the attribute byte it is given (print_hex_digits).
comment print_hex_24_bits Unused: writes a number's low 24 bits as six hex digits on the text layer, as print_hex_halfword does (print_hex_digits).
comment print_hex_word Unused: writes a 32-bit number as eight hex digits on the text layer, as print_hex_halfword does (print_hex_digits, which it runs on into).
comment bcd_to_binary Unused: converts R0's eight decimal digits (four bits each, the highest first) into a number, returned in R0: eight times, the result so far times 10 plus the next digit. The inverse of binary_to_bcd.
result bcd_to_binary R0 value
comment reserve_object_slot Unused: takes the first free object slot as start_object does (FF into its byte of the 7C at screen - 7FF0) and clears 100 bytes for it, but at the slot byte's address with its low half shifted 8 left (201000 + 100 * slot for screen 1), not where start_object puts slot n (screen - 7C00 + 100 * n), and without making it live, its slot number or the frame's list; with no slot free, the spare object at screen - 7F00. Returns the object.
result reserve_object_slot R21 object object
comment free_object_slot Unused: frees the slot of the object R21: clears the slot byte (screen - 7FF0) numbered by bits 8-15 of the object's address, which is not the object's own slot: for start_object's slot n it clears slot n + 4, for reserve_object_slot's slot n slot n + 10.
comment request_second_sound Unused: puts sound R0 in the screen's second ring of 32 sound requests (second_sound_queue_write, the requests from 842; send_sound_requests sends them to the command byte 8 after the first ring's), as request_sound does for the first: while demo_flags bit 8 is set only the sounds the table at 01204E allows, and only with advertise_sound set.
comment road_row_pair_crash_check_unflipped Unused (called only by unused_0129D0, an unused handler that goes on into road_row_pair_draw's code): road_row_pair_crash_check with kind 0 (a crash), but the mirrored item's hit_box is only moved across the road's centre, not flipped: its offsets are added to the mirrored place as they are. For a box even about its middle the two are the same.
comment backdrop_far_start Unused object handler: sets up a sprite on the background as backdrop_near_start does (from its data the animation, palette, panorama_x, height and half_width; draw_mode A, layer 1; straight to state 1 when screen background_at_start is set); then backdrop_far_update.
comment backdrop_far_update Only unused code reaches it (backdrop_far_start): keeps the sprite on the background's panorama at the panorama's own speed (x from the car's view_yaw, a quarter of it until screen background_changing is set, less panorama_x), y from the background's height (screen background_scroll_y) and height. By state: 0 waits until it is off the screen; 1 draw_object while on the screen; off it, it ends once leaving.
comment backdrop_flashing_start Unused object handler: sets up a sprite on the background as backdrop_far_start does, with other_palette 120; then backdrop_flashing_update.
comment backdrop_flashing_update Only unused code reaches it (backdrop_flashing_start): as backdrop_far_update (on the panorama at its speed), animated (animation_advance), and each frame its palette swaps with other_palette, so it flashes between its own palette and palette 120.
comment kanji_text_viewer_update Unused object handler: shows the Japanese texts of the table at 01B254 (1000 of them) by number, a three-digit number chosen with the buttons. By state: 0 stops the tile unpacking (unpack_state), sets up video RAM (init_video) with the tilemaps off and the bitmap layer on at 4 bits a pixel, scroll 0, palette FF (video_registers bit B cleared, video_registers[1] = F, video_registers[68], video_registers[69], video_registers[70]: words 00, 02, 88, 8A and 8C of the video registers' copy, sent as send_video_registers asks), the number 0 (screen route_map_signals-5806), the cursor (x) on the units; 1 buttons_pressed bits 3 and 2 move the cursor over the three digits, bits 1 and 0 step the digit under it (0-9, wrapping); on screen 1 the number is written at text cell (5,10) (kanji_text_viewer_draw_number) with the cursor's digit blinking as '*' (blink_count), and the text drawn on the bitmap a line at a time (kanji_text_viewer_draw_line) from 16 rows and 128 pixels in, 16 rows a line; 2 draws the text only; 3 nothing (nothing sets states 2 and 3).
comment kanji_text_viewer_draw_line Only unused code reaches it (kanji_text_viewer_update): draws a line of the text at R10 on the bitmap layer from R11: each halfword a character (kanji_text_viewer_character_address, kanji_text_viewer_draw_character, 16 pixels on); 0 ends the line, a negative one the text (R4 = -1).
comment kanji_text_viewer_draw_character Only unused code reaches it (kanji_text_viewer_draw_line): draws a 16x16 character on the bitmap layer at R11 from its 32 bytes at R13: four 8x8 quarters (kanji_text_viewer_draw_quarter), upper left, upper right, lower left, lower right.
comment kanji_text_viewer_draw_quarter Only unused code reaches it (kanji_text_viewer_draw_character): draws eight rows of eight one-bit pixels (a byte each, from R13) at R12 on the bitmap layer, 100 bytes a row, each byte spread by the table at 1A6340 into eight 4-bit pixels of pen 1.
comment kanji_text_viewer_character_address Only unused code reaches it (kanji_text_viewer_draw_line): the address of character R13 of the kanji font at 1CF000 (8 bytes a step: a character is 32).
comment kanji_text_viewer_draw_number Only unused code reaches it (kanji_text_viewer_update): writes R4 (0-999) as three decimal digits on the text layer from cell R3 (attribute 60).
comment test_mode_duration_to_seconds Unused: turns a duration kept as five bytes at R10 (seconds, minutes and hours a byte each, then days a halfword) into seconds: ((days * 24 + hours) * 60 + minutes) * 60 + seconds, in R0 (R1 the carry).
result test_mode_duration_to_seconds R0 seconds
comment test_mode_split_minutes_seconds Unused: writes a time of R0 seconds at R10 as two bytes: the seconds (R0 mod 60), then the minutes (R0 / 60).
